# FIFA World Cup awards

This article is about the men's senior tournament. For the women's tournament, see [FIFA Women's World Cup awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Women's_World_Cup_awards).



[Spain](609825.md) players [Pau Cubarsí](74669990.md), [Rodri](48849865.md) and [Unai Simón](58209770.md) receiving awards after the [2026 FIFA World Cup final](75845879.md)

&#x20; At the end of each [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) final tournament, several awards are presented to the players and teams who have distinguished themselves in various aspects of the game.

## Awards

The World Cup winners are symbolically presented with the [FIFA World Cup Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Trophy) after [the final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Final), but it remains in the custody of FIFA between tournaments.[^cite_note-1] The previous [Jules Rimet Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Rimet_Trophy) was permanently awarded to the [Brazilian Football Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Football_Confederation) after [the team](149286.md)'s third championship [in 1970](157227.md).[^cite_note-2] Squad members and coaching staff of the national teams placing first, second, and third in the tournament receive [gold](526488.md), [silver](526647.md), and [bronze](526648.md) medals, respectively.[^cite_note-3] In some tournaments, the fourth-placed team members also received medals after the [third-place playoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third-place_playoff).[^cite_note-4]

There are five post-tournament awards from the FIFA Technical Study Group:[^cite_note-FIFA-2010-5][^cite_note-Fifa-Tech-2018-6]

- the **Golden Ball** (commercially termed "[Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas) Golden Ball") for best player, first awarded in [1982](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_FIFA_World_Cup).

- the **Golden Boot** (commercially termed "Adidas Golden Boot", formerly known as the "Adidas Golden Shoe" from 1982 to 2006) for top goalscorer, first awarded in 1982;

- the **Golden Glove** (commercially termed "Adidas Golden Glove", formerly known as the "[Lev Yashin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lev_Yashin) Award" from 1994 to 2006) for best goalkeeper, first awarded in [1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup);

- the **FIFA Young Player Award** (commercially termed "FIFA Young Player Award, presented by [Aramco](290527.md)"

  [^cite_note-7]

  &#x20;since 2026, formerly known as the "Best Young Player Award" from 2006 to 2010) for best player under 21 years of age at the start of the calendar year, first awarded in [2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup);

- the **FIFA Fair Play Trophy** for the team that advanced to the second round with the best record of fair play, first awarded in [1970](157227.md).

There is one award voted on by fans during the tournament:

- the **Player of the Match&#x20;**&#x20;(commercially termed "[Michelob Ultra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelob_Ultra) Superior Player of the Match"

  [^cite_note-8]

  , formerly known as the "[Budweiser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budweiser) Man of the Match" from 2002 to 2018 and "Budweiser Player of the Match" in 2022) for outstanding performance during each match of the tournament, first awarded in [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup).

There are three awards voted on by fans after the conclusion of the tournament:

- the **Goal of the Tournament**, (commercially termed "[Hyundai](42257.md) Goal of the Tournament") for the fans' best goal scored during the tournament, first awarded in [2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup);

- the **Most Entertaining Team** for the team that has entertained the public the most, during the World Cup final tournament, as determined by a poll of the general public.

- the **Dream Team,** which is considered as a replacement for the All-Star Team. It has been selected by fans in 2002, 2010 to 2018, and 2026.

One other award was given between 1994 and 2006:[^cite_note-USA_Today-2010-9]

- an **All-Star Team** comprising the best players of the tournament chosen by the FIFA Technical Study Group. From 2010 onwards, all Dream Teams or Statistical Teams are unofficial, as reported by FIFA itself.

## Golden Ball



[Lionel Messi](2150841.md) of [Argentina](454699.md) is the only player to win the award twice (2014, 2022).

"FIFA World Cup Golden Ball" redirects here. For the women's award, see [FIFA Women's World Cup Golden Ball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Women's_World_Cup_Golden_Ball).

Not to be confused with the unrelated award of the [same English translation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballon_d'Or).

The **Golden Ball** award is presented to the best player at each FIFA World Cup finals, with a shortlist drawn up by the FIFA technical committee (Technical Study Group) and the winner voted for by representatives of the media.[^cite_note-footballcoal-2022-10] Those who finish as runners-up in the vote receive the *Silver Ball* and *Bronze Ball* awards as the second and third most outstanding players in the tournament respectively. The award was introduced in the 1982 FIFA World Cup, sponsored by [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas) and *[France Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_Football)*.[^cite_note-82debut-11] [Oliver Kahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oliver_Kahn) is to date the only goalkeeper to have won the award, which he did in the 2002 FIFA World Cup. At the 2022 FIFA World Cup, [Lionel Messi](2150841.md) became the first player to win a second Golden Ball.[^cite_note-FIFA.com-2022-12]

### Unofficial winner (1978)

A group of journalists and experts selected the best player of the [1978 tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978_FIFA_World_Cup),[^cite_note-Mundo_Deportivo-1978-14] and their selection is recognised by the [FIFA](11049.md) website. FIFA recognises only this selection besides the Golden Ball award. [Hans Krankl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Krankl) received the same number of votes as [Dirceu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirceu) at the time,[^cite_note-Mundo_Deportivo-1978-14] and was not chosen as the third best player by FIFA later.

| World Cup | Winner | Runner-up | Third place |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [1978 Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978_FIFA_World_Cup) | [Argentina](454699.md) [Mario Kempes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Kempes)[^cite_note-FIFA-2018-15] | [Italy](362466.md) [Paolo Rossi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Rossi)[^cite_note-78WC-16] | [Brazil](149286.md) [Dirceu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirceu)[^cite_note-78WC-16] |

### Contemporary and retrospective selections (1930–1974)

On 2 August 1950, Dr. [Friedebert Becker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friedebert_Becker?action=edit\&redlink=1), the editor-in-chief of [*Kicker*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kicker_\(sports_magazine\)) (then *Sport-Magazin*), chose his best players of the tournament.[^cite_note-Sport_Magazin-1950-17] *[France Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_Football)*, the sponsor of Golden Ball and [Ballon d'Or](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballon_d'Or), selected the best player of the 1966 FIFA World Cup at that time with *[L'Équipe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'%C3%89quipe)*, and [Bobby Charlton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobby_Charlton) became the winner.[^cite_note-FranceFootball-18] In 1998, the Argentinian newspaper *[Clarín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clar%C3%ADn_\(Argentine_newspaper\))* provided a brief description of each star player dating back to 1930. Some are controversial decisions as is the case with retrospective awards. The 1998 winner was awarded after the tournament ended.[^cite_note-Clarin-2018-19]

## Golden Boot

"FIFA World Cup Golden Boot" redirects here. For the women's award, see [FIFA Women's World Cup Golden Boot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Women's_World_Cup_Golden_Boot).

refer to caption

[Kylian Mbappé](48711857.md), captain of [France](250197.md), the only player to win the award twice (2022, 2026).

The **Golden Boot** or **Golden Shoe** award goes to the top goalscorer of the FIFA World Cup. While every World Cup had a ranking of the goalscorers, the first time an award was given was in 1982,[^cite_note-FIFA_Awards-13] under the name Golden Shoe.[^cite_note-82debut-11] It was rechristened Golden Boot in 2010.[^cite_note-glance-37] FIFA sometimes lists the top goalscorers of previous Cups among the Golden Boot winners.[^cite_note-boot-38] If there is more than one player with the same number of goals, since 1994 the tie-breaker goes to the player with more assists. If there is still more than one player, since 2006 the tie is decided by (fewer) minutes played in the tournament. A Silver Boot and a Bronze Boot are also awarded for the second and third-highest goalscorers respectively.

## Golden Glove

"FIFA World Cup Golden Glove" redirects here. For the women's award, see [FIFA Women's World Cup Golden Glove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Women's_World_Cup_Golden_Glove).

The **Golden Glove** award is awarded to the best goalkeeper of the tournament. The award was introduced with the name "[Lev Yashin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lev_Yashin) Award" in 1994, in honour of the late [Soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) goalkeeper.[^cite_note-FIFA_Awards-13] It was rechristened "Golden Glove" in 2010. The FIFA Technical Study Group recognises the top goalkeeper of the tournament based on the player's performance throughout the final competition. Although goalkeepers have this specific award for their position, they are still eligible for the Golden Ball as well, as when Oliver Kahn was awarded in 2002.[^cite_note-74]

## FIFA Young Player Award

"FIFA World Cup Young Player Award" redirects here. For the women's award, see [FIFA Women's World Cup Young Player Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Women's_World_Cup_Young_Player_Award).

The **FIFA Young Player Award** ("Best Young Player Award" 2006–2010) was awarded for the first time at the [2006 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup) in Germany and given to Germany's [Lukas Podolski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lukas_Podolski).[^cite_note-podolski-75] The award is given to the best player in the tournament who is at most 21 years old. For the [2022 World Cup](17742072.md), this meant that the player had to have been born on or after 1 January 2001. In 2006, the election took place on FIFA's official World Cup website with the help of The FIFA Technical Study Group.[^cite_note-76][^cite_note-77]

FIFA organised a survey on the Internet for users to choose the "Best Young Player" of the World Cup, between 1958 and 2002, named the best young player of each tournament.[^cite_note-78] With 61% of the overall vote, the winner was [Pelé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pel%C3%A9), who finished ahead of the [Peruvian](170691.md) [Teófilo Cubillas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Te%C3%B3filo_Cubillas), the best young player at [Mexico 1970](157227.md), and England's [Michael Owen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Owen), who reached similar heights at [France 98](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_FIFA_World_Cup).[^cite_note-79][^cite_note-80] The winner of the award has only been part of the winning nation four times: [Pelé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pel%C3%A9) in [1958](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1958_FIFA_World_Cup), [Kylian Mbappé](48711857.md) in [2018](13327177.md), [Enzo Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enzo_Fern%C3%A1ndez) in [2022](17742072.md), and [Pau Cubarsí](74669990.md) in [2026](36581929.md).

## FIFA Fair Play Trophy

"FIFA World Cup Fair Play Trophy" redirects here. For the women's award, see [FIFA Women's World Cup Fair Play Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Women's_World_Cup_Fair_Play_Trophy).

The **FIFA Fair Play Trophy** is given to the team with the best record of [fair play](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportsmanship) during the World Cup final tournament since 1970. Only teams that qualified for the second round are considered. The winners of this award earn the FIFA Fair Play Trophy, a diploma, a fair play medal for each player and official, and \$50,000 worth of football equipment to be used for youth development.[^cite_note-84] The appearance of the award was originally a certificate. From 1982 to 1990, it was a golden trophy based on [Sport Billy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_Billy), a football-playing cartoon character from 1982 who became an icon for FIFA Fair play.[^cite_note-85][^cite_note-86] Ever since 1994, it is simply a trophy with an elegant footballer figure.[^cite_note-87] [Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peru_national_football_team) was the first nation to win the award after receiving no yellow or red cards in the [1970 FIFA World Cup](157227.md) held in Mexico.[^cite_note-88]



Peru's FIFA Fair Play trophy award. Peru won the award after receiving no yellow or red cards in the tournament.

## Player of the Match

The [Player of the Match](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Player_of_the_Match) (POTM) award picks the outstanding player in every match of the tournament. The award was introduced with the name "Man of the Match" in 2002. It was rechristened "Player of the Match" in 2022. While the inaugural two editions were chosen by the technical group,[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90] since 2010, the Player of the Match is picked by an online poll on FIFA's website.[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93]

## Most Entertaining Team

The **Most Entertaining Team** award was a subjectively awarded prize for the team that had done the most to entertain the public with a positive approach to the game, organised through public participation in a poll[^cite_note-FIFA_Awards-13] starting in 1994 to 2006.[^cite_note-glance-37] It has since been replaced by the All-Star Team and Dream Team.&#x20;

| World Cup | Most Entertaining Team Award |
| --- | --- |
| [1994 United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup) | [Brazil](149286.md)[^cite_note-FIFA.com-63] |
| [1998 France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_FIFA_World_Cup) | [France](250197.md)[^cite_note-FIFA.com-63] |
| [2002 South Korea–Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup) | [South Korea](1018627.md)[^cite_note-94] |
| [2006 Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup) | [Portugal](576328.md)[^cite_note-95] |

## All-Star Team and Dream Team

### Official winners (1994–2006)

The **All-Star Team** is a team of the best performers at the respective World Cup finals. Since 1994, FIFA decided to add official best squads, chosen by its Technical Study Group under the brand name *[MasterCard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MasterCard) All-Star Team*.[^cite_note-94team-96] For 1998 and 2002, a distinction was made between starters and reserve members.

### Other selections

Many different newspapers, sports journalists, managers and former players have picked their All-Star teams for the tournaments over the years. Newspapers which picked their All-Star teams include *[Sport-Magazin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kicker_\(sports_magazine\)), Mundo Esportivo, [France Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_Football)*, [Associated Press](18935732.md), *Estadio, [El Gráfico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Gr%C3%A1fico_\(Argentina\)), Goles, [Guerin Sportivo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guerin_Sportivo), Crónica, [El Mercurio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Mercurio), La Prensa, [Clarín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clar%C3%ADn_\(Argentine_newspaper\)), [La Razón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Raz%C3%B3n_\(Buenos_Aires\)), [El País](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Pa%C3%ADs_\(Uruguay\)), [L'Équipe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'%C3%89quipe), [Mundo Deportivo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mundo_Deportivo), [De Volkskrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Volkskrant), [La Gazzetta dello Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Gazzetta_dello_Sport), [Don Balón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Bal%C3%B3n), [La Stampa](1810129.md), [Kicker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kicker_\(sports_magazine\)), Deporte Gráfico, Spotivo Sur, [Match](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Match_\(magazine\)), Fußball Woche, [Placar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Placar), [Shoot!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoot_\(football_magazine\))*, [BBC Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Sport), *[Süddeutsche Zeitung](30873120.md), [Diario AS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diario_AS)* and [*Marca*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marca_\(newspaper\)).

Sports journalists, managers and former players who picked their All-Star teams include Dr. Friedebert Becker, [Gabriel Hanot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Hanot), [Enzo Bearzot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enzo_Bearzot), [Pelé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pel%C3%A9), [Roberto Bettega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Bettega), [Eugenio Bersellini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugenio_Bersellini), [Giovanni Trapattoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Trapattoni), [Massimo Giacomini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massimo_Giacomini), [Sandro Mazzola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandro_Mazzola), Paolo Carosi, [Jimmy Greaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Greaves), [Pichi Alonso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pichi_Alonso) and [Johan Cruyff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johan_Cruyff).

Since the beginning many newspapers gave players points to players based on their performances at the World Cup. Some are based in statistics, but some were just rankings given by experts.[^cite_note-castrol_2010-06-28-100] [Castrol Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castrol_performance_index) has given retrospective rankings to players until 1966 with their expert panel consisting of people such as [Cristiano Ronaldo](623737.md), [Arsène Wenger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ars%C3%A8ne_Wenger), [Marcel Desailly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcel_Desailly), [Cafu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cafu), [Alan Shearer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Shearer), [Emilio Butragueño](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emilio_Butrague%C3%B1o), [Ronald Koeman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Koeman), [Pierluigi Collina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierluigi_Collina), [Ottmar Hitzfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottmar_Hitzfeld), [Gary Bailey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Bailey), [Peter Stöger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_St%C3%B6ger), [Mohamed Al-Deayea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Al-Deayea) and [Stanislav Levý](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanislav_Lev%C3%BD).[^cite_note-101]

There are rumours about "official" World Cup All-Star teams across the internet. The same teams have been published on many websites including [football.sporting99.com](https://football.sporting99.com/fifa-world-cup-all-star-team.html), [worldcupbrazil.net](https://web.archive.org/web/20200211160635/http://www.worldcupbrazil.net/world-cup-history/), [talksport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talksport)[^cite_note-102] and [thesoccerworldcups.com](https://www.thesoccerworldcups.com/world_cups.php). It is unclear where these teams originated, but the earliest they can be found on the internet was in the October of 2011 on the football.sporting99.com website.[^cite_note-103]

#### Unofficial winners (1990)

In 1990, there was an All-Star Team announced in combination with the Golden Ball ceremony. It was chosen by the same journalists who chose the best player, but this team is still considered unofficial.[^cite_note-150][^cite_note-BigSoccer-deadlink-151]

#### Fans' winners (Dream Team) (2002, 2010–2018, 2026)

In 2002, the tournament's sponsor MasterCard announced fans' All-Star Team based on the results of an online poll as well as official All-Star Team.[^cite_note-152] Between 2010 and 2018, the *Fan Dream Team* was voted by online poll of FIFA website. FIFA explained these are not official.[^cite_note-fantasy-153] The *Dream XI* was voted for by fans in an online poll in 2026.[^cite_note-dreamXI-154]

#### Statistical winners (2010–2018)

After FIFA changed its sponsor from MasterCard to [Visa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visa_Inc.) in 2007,[^cite_note-USA_Today-2010-9] it published Team of the Tournament based on statistical data of other sponsors as well, which evaluates players' performances. FIFA explained these are not official.[^cite_note-fantasy-153]

## Goal of the Tournament

The **Goal of the Tournament** award was awarded for the first time at the [2006 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup).[^cite_note-180]

- Scores and results list the goal tally of the players' team first.

### Official winners

| World Cup | Player | Scored against | Score | Minute | Result | Round | Ref. |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [2006 Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup) | [Argentina](454699.md) [Maxi Rodríguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maxi_Rodr%C3%ADguez) | [Mexico](808402.md) | **2**–1 | 98' ([e.t.](633242.md#Association_football)) | 2–1 ([a.e.t.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extra_time_\(association_football\))) | [Round of 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup_knockout_stage#Argentina_vs_Mexico) | [^cite_note-181] |
| [2010 South Africa](168079.md) | [Uruguay](679782.md) [Diego Forlán](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diego_Forl%C3%A1n) | [Germany](250204.md) | **2**–1 | 51' | 2–3 | [Match for third place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_FIFA_World_Cup_knockout_stage#Match_for_third_place) |  |
| [2014 Brazil](656933.md) | [Colombia](680175.md) [James Rodríguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Rodr%C3%ADguez) | [Uruguay](679782.md) | **1**–0 | 28' | 2–0 | [Round of 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_World_Cup_knockout_stage#Colombia_vs_Uruguay) |  |
| [2018 Russia](13327177.md) | [France](250197.md) [Benjamin Pavard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Pavard) | [Argentina](454699.md) | **2**–2 | 57' | 4–3 | [Round of 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_knockout_stage#France_vs_Argentina) |  |
| [2022 Qatar](17742072.md) | [Brazil](149286.md) [Richarlison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richarlison) | [Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_national_football_team) | **2**–0 | 73' | 2–0 | [Group stage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_G#Brazil_vs_Serbia) | [^cite_note-FIFA-182] |
| [2026 Canada–Mexico–United States](36581929.md) | [Cape Verde](1205575.md) [Sidny Lopes Cabral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sidny_Lopes_Cabral) | [Argentina](454699.md) | **2**–2 | 103' ([e.t.](633242.md#Association_football)) | 2–3 ([a.e.t.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extra_time_\(association_football\))) | [Round of 32](81667041.md#Argentina_vs_Cape_Verde) | [^cite_note-2026Winner-183] |

## External links

[^cite_note-42]: &#x20;FIFA initially credited Nejedlý with only four goals, which would make him joint top scorer with [Angelo Schiavio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelo_Schiavio) of [Italy](362466.md) and [Edmund Conen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmund_Conen) of [Germany](250204.md). However, FIFA changed it to five goals in November 2006, making Nejedlý the outright top scorer.<sup>[\[41\]](#cite_note-hattrick-41)</sup>

[^cite_note-43]: &#x20;FIFA initially credited Leônidas with eight goals. However, in November 2006, FIFA confirmed that in the quarter-final tie against Czechoslovakia, he had scored once, not twice as FIFA had originally recorded, meaning he had scored only seven goals in total.<sup>[\[41\]](#cite_note-hattrick-41)</sup>

[^cite_note-46]: &#x20;There was controversy regarding the number of goals Brazilian Ademir had scored in 1950, as a result of incomplete data concerning the Final Round game Brazil vs. Spain (6–1). The 5–0 goal had been credited to Jair, but is now credited to Ademir.<sup>[\[42\]](#cite_note-44)</sup><sup>[\[43\]](#cite_note-45)</sup>

[^cite_note-silver1978note-48]: &#x20;Although Cubillas and Rensenbrink scored the same number of goals, the FIFA website retrospectively credits them with silver and bronze balls respectively.<sup>[\[44\]](#cite_note-silver1978refs-47)</sup>

[^cite_note-50]: &#x20;Salenko is the only player to win the award playing for a team that was eliminated in the group stage. His six goals are the only international goals he ever scored.

[^cite_note-52]: &#x20;Despite the assist tiebreaker, Salenko and Stoichkov remained tied with six goals and one assist each, and both received the Golden Shoe.<sup>[\[46\]](#cite_note-busw1994-51)</sup>

[^cite_note-54]: &#x20;Romário and Andersson surpassed the other two players with five goals ([Jürgen Klinsmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%BCrgen_Klinsmann) and [Roberto Baggio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Baggio)) by having three assists each.<sup>[\[46\]](#cite_note-busw1994-51)</sup><sup>[\[47\]](#cite_note-53)</sup>

[^cite_note-57]: &#x20;Šuker and Batistuta each had three assists.<sup>[\[48\]](#cite_note-awards1998-55)</sup> No other player scored five goals.<sup>[\[49\]](#cite_note-56)</sup>

[^cite_note-60]: &#x20;During the tournament, after the group stage match against Costa Rica, Ronaldo logged a protest against the crediting of a goal as an own goal, and FIFA granted him the change.<sup>[\[51\]](#cite_note-59)</sup>

[^cite_note-62]: &#x20;Klose and Rivaldo each had one assist.<sup>[\[50\]](#cite_note-awards2002-58)</sup> No other player scored five goals.<sup>[\[52\]](#cite_note-61)</sup>

[^cite_note-Crespo_and_Ronaldo-65]: &#x20;Eight players had scored three goals.<sup>[\[54\]](#cite_note-topgoals2006-64)</sup> Ronaldo, Crespo and [Zinedine Zidane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinedine_Zidane) stood out for having one assist, and then the two recipients were determined by less playtime (308 minutes for Crespo, 411 for Ronaldo, 559 for Zidane).<sup>[\[53\]](#cite_note-FIFA.com-63)</sup><sup>[\[54\]](#cite_note-topgoals2006-64)</sup> (The five players with three goals and no assists were [David Villa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Villa), [Fernando Torres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Torres), [Maxi Rodriguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maxi_Rodriguez), [Lukas Podolski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lukas_Podolski), [Thierry Henry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thierry_Henry).<sup>[\[54\]](#cite_note-topgoals2006-64)</sup>)

[^cite_note-Müller,_Villa,_Sneijder_and_Forlán-67]: &#x20;Müller, Villa, Sneijder and [Diego Forlán](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diego_Forl%C3%A1n) tied with five goals. Müller won by virtue of having more assists (three) than the rest (each had one). Villa won the Silver Boot due to playing fewer minutes than Sneijder, and Sneijder won the Bronze Boot due to having played fewer minutes than Forlán.<sup>[\[55\]](#cite_note-66)</sup>

[^cite_note-69]: &#x20;Neymar, [Lionel Messi](2150841.md) and [Robin van Persie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robin_van_Persie) all had four goals in the tournament. Neymar received the Bronze Boot for playing fewer minutes than his competitors (480; Messi played 693 minutes, and Van Persie, 548).<sup>[\[56\]](#cite_note-fifa_2014-06-03-68)</sup>

[^cite_note-Griezmann,_Lukaku,_\[\[Denis_Cheryshev\]\],_\[\[Cristiano_Ronaldo\]\]_and_\[\[Kylian_Mbappé\]\]-70]: &#x20;Griezmann, Lukaku, [Denis Cheryshev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Cheryshev), [Cristiano Ronaldo](623737.md) and [Kylian Mbappé](48711857.md) tied with four goals. In the assists tiebreaker, Griezmann won the Silver Boot by virtue of having two, while Lukaku got the Bronze Boot as he had one. The rest had zero.<sup>[\[56\]](#cite_note-fifa_2014-06-03-68)</sup>

[^cite_note-Giroud_and_Julián_Alvarez-72]: &#x20;Giroud and [Julián Alvarez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juli%C3%A1n_Alvarez) tied with four goals. Giroud won the Bronze Boot due to playing fewer minutes than Alvarez.<sup>[\[57\]](#cite_note-71)</sup>

[^cite_note-73]: &#x20;Bellingham and [Erling Haaland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erling_Haaland) tied with seven goals. Bellingham won the Bronze Boot by virtue of having more assists (one) than Haaland (zero).

[Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20221128203321/https://sports.yahoo.com/mystery-solved-players-get-win-world-cup-215739918.html) from the original on 28 November 2022. Retrieved 13 June 2026.
